Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 2 years and 0 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 2 years and 0 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 2 years and 0 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Delusions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria, psychotic_symptoms, depressive_episode, delusions_and_hallucinations\nAnswer strategy: Explain the timeline clearly based on the profile: state that your overall struggles have been going on for about 2 years, with depressive symptoms being present for most of that time. Clarify that the delusional thoughts and hallucinations started more recently, about 8 months ago. Mention that there was a period of about two weeks or more where you experienced the hallucinations and delusions without feeling depressed, but currently (for the past 2 months), both the depression and the psychotic symptoms are occurring at the same time."

My family says these things aren't real, but they feel completely real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your mood, such as feelings of depression or periods of unusually elevated or irritable mood?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been feeling really down and depressed for about the last two months, and I've lost almost all my interest and motivation to do anything. I haven't had any times where I felt unusually high-energy, excited, or irritable, though. It has mostly just been this heavy, low feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these troubling thoughts about being watched and things having hidden meanings, and have they occurred at the same time as your depressive symptoms or at different times?\n\nPatient: I've been experiencing these thoughts for about eight months now. They've been happening during my depression these past two months, but I also had them before that. There was even a period of a few weeks where I had these thoughts but didn't feel depressed at all.\n\nDoctor: Have these delusional thoughts or your depressive symptoms significantly impacted your ability to work, maintain relationships, or perform daily activities, and if so, to what extent?\n\nPatient: Yes, they've completely disrupted my life. I've lost all motivation to work or even handle basic daily tasks, so I've just stopped doing them. I've also completely withdrawn from my family and friends because I feel entirely disconnected and lack the desire to maintain any relationships.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any notable difficulties with organizing your thoughts, making decisions, or exhibiting bizarre behaviors that are not directly related to your delusional thoughts or depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had extreme difficulty making even simple daily decisions, like what to wear or eat. My thinking also feels very foggy and dazed, which makes it really hard to keep my thoughts organized. I'm not really sure about any bizarre behaviors, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any hallucinations, such as hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there, in addition to your delusional thoughts and depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been hearing whispering voices and seeing shadowy figures when no one else is around. Sometimes I even feel strange sensations on my skin or smell things that others say aren't there. It's incredibly exhausting and scary to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the timeline of when these hallucinations started in relation to your delusional thoughts and depressive symptoms, and have they been constant or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: The hallucinations started about eight months ago alongside the delusional thoughts, and they tend to come and go. I actually had a period of a few weeks back then where I experienced them without feeling depressed, even though I've struggled with depression off and on for about two years now. Currently, for the past two months, the hallucinations and the depression have been happening at the same time.\n\nPatient: The hallucinations started about eight months ago alongside the delusional thoughts, and they tend to come and go.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 2 years and 0 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 2 years and 0 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Delusions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria\nAnswer strategy: Acknowledge the doctor's conclusion and diagnostic impression of Schizoaffective Disorder (Depressive Type). Express appreciation for their evaluation, confirm that the explanation of concurrent depressive and psychotic symptoms makes sense given your experience, and state your willingness to proceed with any further recommended evaluation or treatment plans."

Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.
